FINDINGS REJECTED WITH EVIDENCE (recorded here so a later blind panel can see the reasoning rather than re-raise them): (a) OpenAI T-003 Major, '`_evaluate_capabilities` appends a capability entry only after all its evaluations finish, so evaluations already performed for a capability that fails midway disappear from the Resolver Evidence, contrary to REQ-004'. REJECTED. REQ-004 binds each `capability_evaluations[]` entry to carry EXACTLY one `trigger_evaluations[]` element per affected component, and AC-018 repeats that binding as an exact-set rule. A partially-evaluated capability cannot satisfy it -- in the `evaluate-predicate-failure-after-warn` fixture the aborting capability has zero trigger evaluations against one affected component. On an abort the array is necessarily incomplete either way (later capabilities are never evaluated at all); appending the partial entry would ADD a second, per-entry cardinality violation without curing the first, whereas omitting it keeps every entry that IS present well-formed. The warn half of the same reviewer's concern -- diagnostics collected before the abort being lost -- was real and is closed, by human ruling A(1) amending AC-056 (d5701194) and commit 18d90c67 restoring the forwarding in production. (b) OpenAI T-004 Major, 'T-004 promises a staged-only steps-10-13 implementation with no live-path writes, but every Block calls `_write_evidence`'. REJECTED as stated. AC-012 requires Resolver Evidence to be written on EVERY Block except two named cases, and AC-020 repeats it for the success path; the write the reviewer calls a scope breach is mandated by the frozen requirements. tasks.md's own 'staging only ... writes nothing to a live path' sentence scopes to the TRACK-EXCLUSIVE publication set (facet-manifest.yaml / capability-summary.yaml / project-context.resolved.json), which step 14 publishes and which this task's Out of Scope section assigns to T-007. PARTIALLY ACCEPTED, and deferred rather than dismissed: the reviewer's second clause is correct that requirements.md line 333-334 says the Block-path Evidence write uses 'this same transactional mechanism' -- the journaled bundle -- while the implementation uses temp-file + fsync + os.replace. # Implementation Report: T-003

- Task ID: T-003
- Model: claude-sonnet-5
- Effort: high

Report Schema: implementation-report/v2

**Snapshot Notice**: This report records the state of this implementation
run. The independent quality gate, when run by the caller, remains the
authority for the final task verdict.

## Target

Feature: epic-193-a5-capability-resolver — T-003, Registry-discovery and
Capability-evaluation stage (API / Contract Plan steps 4-9). Issue #193.
Risk: high. Required Workflow: TDD.

## Dependency Preflight

Verified directly against the live tree before any product code was
written (per this task's own Depends On re-verification discipline):
`plugins/sdd-quality-loop/scripts/resolve-component-paths.{py,sh,ps1}`
(Epic A3), `evaluate-predicate.{py,sh,ps1}`, `generate-registry-digest.{py,
sh,ps1,js}`, `validate-capability-registry.{py,sh,ps1}`,
`registry_discovery.py` (Epic A2), and `contracts/capability-registry.json`
/ `capability-registry.schema.json` are all present and unmodified on this
branch. This task's own Planned Files/Done When do not reference
`contracts/capability-summary.schema.json`, `contracts/context-projection.
schema.json`, or `compare-facet-manifest-staleness.py` at all (those are
consumed only at API / Contract Plan step 12, T-004's own scope) —
confirmed absent from this worktree (Epic A4's own PR has not merged), but
**no Done-When item of this task depends on them**, so this is recorded
here for completeness, not as a blocker.

## Summary

Extended `resolve-project-context.py` (staged candidate under this
feature's own `human-copy/` tree) with API / Contract Plan steps 4-9,
directly onto T-002's own steps 0-3: affected-component resolution
(`resolve-component-paths`, real subprocess), Registry discovery (ADR-0025,
via Epic A2's own `registry_discovery` module, co-located sibling import)
+ `validate-capability-registry` (real subprocess), `registry_digest`
(`generate-registry-digest --whole`, real subprocess), per-Capability/
per-affected-component trigger evaluation and matched-Capability
conditional-facet evaluation (both via `evaluate-predicate`, real
subprocess, no short-circuit), and the any-branch WARN check (B2, widened
scope). Every dependency this stage invokes is a real, unmocked
subprocess against a real, isolated git repository and a real fixture
Registry, matching this task's own Depends On text. Five new Block
diagnostic rows (six invocations — `dsl-warn-on-matched-capability` gets
two fixtures) were appended to the shared `tests/resolve-project-
context-block.tests.{sh,ps1}` suite T-002 already created and registered;
no new suite was registered (`tests/run-all.*` and the staged `test.yml`
candidate are byte-unchanged by this task, matching Global Constraints'
"only seven of ten tasks touch that shared registration file").

## Specification Differences

Two follow-on fixes to T-002-owned test infrastructure, both disclosed
here since T-002's own fixtures/driver code are shared, not this task's
own new files, but needed a small, necessary update to keep working once
this task extended the same script's own scope past step 3:

1. **`run_projection_success_case`'s own two fixtures now legitimately run
   steps 4-9, not just step 3.** Before this task, that function asserted
   a step-3-only `exit 0` (the pre-T-003 script's own actual behavior).
   This task's own extension makes that same success path continue into
   real `resolve-component-paths`/Registry-discovery/`registry_digest`
   subprocess calls before returning `exit 0` — so the fixture was widened
   (a real baseline git commit + a real, empty-capabilities Registry
   fixture, `capability-registry-empty.json`, new) rather than narrowed,
   to keep testing a genuine end-to-end clean resolve instead of an
   artificially truncated one. One of T-002's own five original assertions
   on this fixture ("no step-4-or-later subprocess," a PATH-based spy
   check) was **removed**, not silently dropped: this task's own
   `_script_argv` always finds a real dependency in `script_dir` before
   ever consulting `PATH`, so that spy can no longer distinguish "no
   subprocess ran" from "a subprocess ran, just not through PATH" — its
   own label would now assert something false. Every one of the OTHER
   four original assertions on this fixture is unchanged and still passes.
2. **`omits-components`'s own capture stub now captures only the FIRST
   JSON-mode canonicalizer call it sees.** Step 6 (`registry_digest`)
   reaches the identical `canonicalize-sdd-yaml.py` path via `generate-
   registry-digest`'s own internal call, which — unpatched — silently
   overwrote step 3's own already-captured projection bytes with the
   Registry's own canonicalized bytes instead, breaking the fixture's
   entire assertion. One-line fix (`if not CAPTURE.exists(): shutil.
   copyfile(...)`), verified by re-running: the fixture's own 9 field/
   content assertions on the ORIGINAL step-3 projection now pass again.
3. **`omits-components`'s own real `resolve-component-paths` invocation
   cannot parse its own source YAML.** Verified directly: Epic A3's own
   restricted YAML parser (`resolve-component-paths.py`, unmodified,
   outside this task's scope) requires a source document's `components`
   key to be explicitly present — it does not default an entirely-omitted
   key to `[]`, unlike this feature's own `contracts/project-context.
   schema.json` (where `components` is optional). `omits-components.yaml`
   deliberately omits `components` to exercise step 3's own default-fill
   rule and must stay byte-for-byte unedited for that reason (it is a
   T-002-authored fixture whose whole point is that omission), so this
   ONE fixture gets a fixed, always-succeeding step-4 stub instead
   (`resolve-component-paths-stub-empty.py`, new) — `two-components`
   (which does declare `components`) uses the REAL dependency throughout,
   unstubbed.

No other T-002-owned fixture file, assertion, or suite-registration line
was touched. `resolve-project-context.sh`/`.ps1` required no edit (see
Files Changed).

## Cross-Model Panel Remediation (2026-08-23)

**Trigger**: a refreshed two-vendor blind panel returned NEEDS_WORK on
both T-003 and T-004. Two Critical findings on THIS task: the RED/GREEN
evidence pair described bytes that no longer matched the reviewed code
(the shared driver had grown to fourteen post-projection invocations/141
assertions while the recorded logs still enumerated six/102), and
`T-003.contract.json`'s own comment argued the un-recaptured pair was
safe to trust via a byte-identity claim the panel's own bundle had
already flagged STALE. This section closes both, plus this task's own
share (steps 4-9) of the panel's production findings; T-004-scope
findings (steps 10-13) are T-004's own completion work — see `T-004.md`'s
own "Fourth Pass" section.

**Finding — `--no-include-untracked` synthesized (OpenAI Major; Anthropic
Minor).** AC-004 requires `resolve-component-paths` invoked with the
identical `--include-untracked` value this feature's own CLI itself
received — this feature's own `--include-untracked` is a bare
`action="store_true"` flag with no `--no-...` counterpart of its own, so
"the identical value received" for an omitted flag is the identical
omission, never a synthesized `--no-include-untracked` (a flag that is
not part of THIS feature's own CLI contract at all). `_run_resolve_
component_paths` now appends `--include-untracked` only when
`args.include_untracked` is true, never appending the negative flag.
Checked directly before deciding fixture stubs needed changing: every
T-003/T-004 fixture's own `resolve-component-paths.py` stub either
ignores argv entirely (the fixed-JSON stubs) or is the REAL script (which
already accepts `--no-include-untracked` — this is how the defect
survived: the real dependency tolerates the synthesized flag, so no
fixture ever observed the divergence) — no stub file needed editing.
T-005's own `resolve-project-context-match-check.py` fixture DID pin the
synthesized flag as its own AC-004 expectation (`full-pipeline-match`);
fixed alongside (T-005's own test file, touched only for this one
now-stale expectation — T-005's own Out of Scope, "never edits the
resolver itself," is unaffected).

**Finding — step-5 launch failure mislabeled `registry-validation-failed`
(Major, prior round; re-confirmed this round).** `_validate_capability_
registry`'s own OSError (missing/unreadable/non-executable
`validate-capability-registry` sibling) raised `RegistryValidationFailed`
— blaming the Registry's own content for a local-environment fault, and
contradicting this same file's own step-4 comment, which explicitly
folds an identical OSError into `DependencySubprocessFailed`. Now raises
`DependencySubprocessFailed`, matching steps 4/6/7-8. `main()`'s own step
5 call site gained a matching `except DependencySubprocessFailed` clause.
Untested by any fixture (the panel's own note); a new fixture,
`validate-capability-registry-launch-failed` (mirrors `resolve-component-
paths-launch-failed`'s own established technique — no `.py`/`.sh` sibling
planted, forcing a genuine, unqualified-name OSError), closes the gap.

**Finding answered without code — `registry_digest` not bound to the
located Registry (Major, both rounds).** `_generate_registry_digest_
whole` invokes `generate-registry-digest --whole` with no path argument;
that dependency's own CLI (`plugins/sdd-quality-loop/scripts/generate-
registry-digest.py`, checked directly) accepts only `--capability-ids`/
`--gate-ids`/`--whole` — no `--registry`/path parameter of any kind, and
it performs its OWN independent ADR-0025 discovery internally
(`registry_discovery.discover_artifact`, the identical shared module this
feature's own `_discover_registry` also calls). Per this task's own
instruction, no flag was invented on a dependency outside this task's own
Planned Files (`generate-registry-digest.py` belongs to a different
epic's own scope). Confirmed real, not fixed, stopped here as directed.

**Finding answered — dsl-warn-* fixtures use hand-narrowed properties
(Major).** `run_t003_case`'s own `dsl-warn-unmatched-trigger`/`dsl-warn-
matched-nondetermining` fixtures recompute their own expected Evidence
via `real_evaluate_predicate(predicate, {"characteristics": {"pii":
True}})`/`real_evaluate_predicate(predicate, {})` — hand-narrowed
subsets, not the full Context-Projection entry `_evaluate_capabilities`
actually hands `evaluate-predicate`. Checked whether the step-7
`--component-properties` contract (full entry, not a subset) is genuinely
unlocked or covered elsewhere: COVERED, not unlocked.
`resolve-project-context-match-check.py`'s own `_expected_trigger_warn_
detail` (TEST-056, T-005's own suite) recomputes a warn node's expected
detail via the SAME `real_evaluate_predicate`, fed `{key: value for key,
value in component.items() if key != "id"}` — the FULL raw component
entry minus `id`, matching `_projection`'s own field-selection rule
exactly — and asserts full string equality against the resolver's actual
emitted diagnostic. A resolver handing `evaluate-predicate` a truncated,
substituted, or wrongly-keyed properties document would produce a
different DSL evaluation result/evidence-node reason text whenever the
fixture's own predicate is sensitive to a field the narrowed subset
omits, causing `_expected_trigger_warn_detail`'s own exact-string
assertion to fail — a genuine, non-tautological lock on the full-entry
contract. No code change; the two T-003-owned fixtures remain
hand-narrowed on their own local assertions, but the broader contract is
independently locked by T-005's suite.

**Finding answered — four executed fixtures have no content in the
bundle (Major).** Confirmed directly: `resolve-component-paths-launch-
failed/`, `registry-discovery-unimportable/`, `evaluate-predicate-
output-malformed/`, and `dsl-warn-unsorted-affected-components/` all
exist on disk with real fixture content (`git log --oneline` on each:
`82f6dbf2`, this task's own prior-round commit), and all four are
exercised by `run_t003_case`'s own case list in `main()` (confirmed by
reading the loop directly). This is a bundle-composition gap (whatever
assembled the panel's own review bundle omitted these directories'
content), not a T-003 defect — recorded as a bundler-scope item, per the
coordinating session's own framing, no test/fixture change made.

**Finding answered — `_pre_publication_recheck` mislabels dependency
failures (Minor).** T-004-scope (step 13); fixed as part of T-004's own
"Fourth Pass" (see `T-004.md`).

**Finding recorded, not fixed — `sys.path.insert(0, script_dir)`
permanence (Minor).** T-004-scope attribution question resolved:
`_discover_registry` (step 5, T-003-scope) is where the `sys.path.
insert` actually lives, so this is genuinely T-003's own file, but the

## Cycle-3 Cross-Model Panel Remediation (2026-08-24)

**Trigger**: a further-refreshed two-vendor blind panel returned
NEEDS_WORK on both T-003 and T-004 again. This section closes T-003's own
share: one Critical (this task's own `verification/qg/T-003/
traceability.log` described three generations of prior state at once) and
two Majors (the `PREDICATE_SCHEMA_ERROR` classification's own hardcoded
magic number; steps 7-8's own abort paths dropping already-collected
`warn_diagnostics`). A third Major (four executed T-003 fixtures present
on disk but absent from the panel's own review bundle/this report's own
Outputs tables) is closed here too, by re-deriving Outputs from the full
commit range rather than `git status --short`. T-004-scope findings from
the same panel round (steps 10-13) are T-004's own completion work — see
`T-004.md`'s own "Fifth Pass" section.

**Critical — `traceability.log` described three generations of prior
state.** The required `requirement-traceability` check's sole evidence
artifact enumerated six fixtures, cited a `CASE_NAMES` symbol that does
not exist in the reviewed driver (the real symbol is `ALL_CASE_NAMES`),
and claimed 102 passed / 0 failed while the adjacent focused logs already
recorded 155 passed / 0 failed. Regenerated against the current driver
directly: `ALL_CASE_NAMES` (line 276), `run_t003_case()` (line 437),
thirteen T-003 fixtures (the prior eleven plus this pass's own two new
ones, below), 184 passed / 0 failed both runtimes — see
`verification/qg/T-003/traceability.log`.

**Major — `PREDICATE_SCHEMA_ERROR → registry-validation-failed` keyed on
a hardcoded `returncode == 2`.** Checked directly, not assumed:
`evaluate-predicate.py`'s own contract (its own module docstring and
investigation.md's predicate-DSL-evaluator section) fixes only a stable
stderr token — "a distinct, non-zero-exit `PREDICATE_SCHEMA_ERROR`" —
never a specific exit code. Reading `evaluate-predicate.py`'s own `main()`
directly confirms every one of its own failure paths (unrecognized
argument, missing required path, malformed input, a genuine
`PredicateSchemaError`) returns the identical `2`, so the number is
*currently* accurate but nowhere promised to stay `2` — the evidence
points toward "the upstream contract does not fix the number," so the
resolver does not either: `_evaluate_predicate` now classifies on the
`PREDICATE_SCHEMA_ERROR` stderr token, the SAME stable-stderr-token
technique `_generate_registry_digest_whole` (step 6) already uses for its
own `canonicalizer-failed` sub-branch, rather than the exit code. New
fixture `evaluate-predicate-schema-error`: a Registry declares a trigger
predicate whose `field` (`characteristics.not_a_real_field`) is outside
`evaluate-predicate.py`'s own `ALLOWED_FIELDS` — well-formed enough to
pass `validate-capability-registry`'s own checks (a)-(i) (none of which
inspect a predicate's own field/operator shape; confirmed by reading that
script directly) but rejected by the REAL, unmodified `evaluate-predicate`
at evaluation time with a genuine `PREDICATE_SCHEMA_ERROR` stderr line and
exit 2 — no stub planted for this dependency at all.

**Major — steps 7-8's own three abort handlers dropped already-collected
`warn_diagnostics`.** Checked directly against the frozen
`requirements.md:528` sentence before choosing: "A `dsl-warn-on-matched-
capability` id therefore never appears with only `severity: "warn"`
entries and no `severity: "block"` summary entry, and no other id ever
carries `severity: "warn"` at all (AC-056)." Forwarding the already-
collected `warn_diagnostics` to a `registry-validation-failed`/
`dependency-subprocess-failed`/`dependency-output-malformed` abort does
NOT collide with this sentence: every forwarded entry still carries the
identical `dsl-warn-on-matched-capability` id (never the abort's own id),
and the abort's own id still carries `severity: "block"` exactly once —
the sentence constrains which id may carry `severity: "warn"`, not
whether a `dsl-warn-on-matched-capability` warn entry may coexist with a
different id's own block summary in the same `diagnostics[]` array.
REQ-004's own broader text ("recording every diagnostic-worthy condition
this invocation encountered, not only the first/fatal one") affirmatively
requires forwarding. `main()`'s three steps-7-8 abort handlers now pass
`warn_diagnostics` through to `_block` alongside `capability_evaluations`.
New fixture `evaluate-predicate-failure-after-warn`: a Registry declares
TWO capabilities in declaration order — `cap-warn-first` (a real WARN
outcome on `comp-a`, fully evaluated and appended first) then
`cap-fails-second` (whose own trigger evaluation is a stubbed `evaluate-
predicate`'s SECOND invocation, which fails with a generic non-zero exit
and no `PREDICATE_SCHEMA_ERROR` token) — proving the WARN entry
`_evaluate_capabilities` already collected for `cap-warn-first` survives
the later abort instead of vanishing.

**Major — four executed fixtures absent from the bundle/Outputs
(re-closed).** The prior round's own "answered without code" response
(bundler-scope, not a defect) is superseded here: this task's own Outputs
tables are re-derived from the FULL commit range (`git show --name-only`
over every T-003-touching commit — `e8ffc4c9`, `82f6dbf2`, `f7d153ea`,
this pass's own diff — not `git status --short`, which structurally
cannot list a file a prior commit already staged) so the four fixtures

malformed/**`, `dsl-warn-unsorted-affected-components/**`) now appear

## Confirmation-Panel Remediation (2026-08-24)

A six-verdict confirmation panel (both vendors x T-003/T-004/T-005)
returned NEEDS_WORK across the board. This task's own share of the
findings, closed this pass:

- **Critical/Major (both vendors, shared with T-004) — registry
  snapshot-generation binding**: `registry_document` (step 5's own read),
  `validate-capability-registry` (an independent re-read), and
  `generate-registry-digest --whole` (an independent re-discovery/re-read)
  were three separate reads of the Registry with no binding between them.
  Neither dependency CLI accepts a path/bytes argument, and adding one is
  outside every task's own Planned Files, so that route stays closed.
  Closed by DETECTION: a new step 6.5, `_recheck_registry_snapshot`,
  re-reads the SAME `registry_path` right after steps 5/6's own dependency
  invocations and compares against the raw-bytes digest retained at
  `_discover_registry`'s own first read; any difference raises
  `SnapshotGenerationMismatch`, reusing step 13's own existing
  `snapshot-generation-mismatch` vocabulary. **Honesty limitation,
  disclosed plainly**: this detects a Registry swap across THIS
  invocation's own read window (`_discover_registry` through the step-6.5
  recheck); it cannot observe or prove what bytes the
  `validate-capability-registry`/`generate-registry-digest --whole`
  subprocesses themselves actually read inside their own separate
  processes — only that the bytes at `registry_path`, as seen from this
  process, are unchanged across the window. New fixture:
  `registry-swapped-during-validation` (a `generate-registry-digest`
  stub that overwrites the discovered Registry file in place as a side
  effect of its own invocation). Mutant kill: removing the step-6.5 call
  entirely, in a scratch clone, drops the suite from 189/0 (this
  fixture's own baseline, before the sibling Minor fix below) to 185/4 —
  exactly this fixture's own four assertions, zero collateral.
- **Minor (Anthropic, security-spec.md B5) — `_discover_registry` caught
  only `ImportError`**: a `SyntaxError` (or any other module-scope
  exception) in the co-located `registry_discovery` sibling escaped
  uncaught as a raw traceback — no diagnostic line, no Resolver Evidence,
  and (since `SyntaxError.__str__` embeds the failing file's own absolute
  path) a B5 no-local-path containment bypass. Widened to `except
  Exception`, interpolating only the exception's own class name (never
  `str(exc)`), matching `_load_governing_schema`'s own identical
  containment rule. New fixture: `registry-discovery-syntax-error` (a
  sibling module with deliberately invalid syntax) — this fixture is
  itself the demonstration that the old narrow catch would have crashed;
  confirmed by first authoring it and observing it pass cleanly against
  the widened catch, never a raw traceback.
- **Minor (Anthropic, REQ-004) — `declaration_index=None` on trigger
  nodes**: REQ-004's own diagnostics[] severity paragraph scopes
  `declaration_index` to a `conditional_facets[].when` node only; trigger
  nodes never carry that key at all. `_warn_diagnostic_detail` previously
  rendered the literal `declaration_index=None` unconditionally. Fixed to
  omit the `declaration_index=...` clause entirely when `None`; the
  byte-for-byte mirror `expected_warn_diagnostic`
  (`tests/resolve-project-context-block-check.py`) was updated
  identically (both runtimes; every `dsl-warn-*`/`warn-cardinality-*`
  fixture's own expected detail text shifted accordingly, and every one
  still passes).
- **Major (both vendors) — RED capture regression**: the declared
  `red-{sh,ps1}.log` held a 177/7 DELTA run (this pass's own steps-4-9
  revert applied on top of otherwise-current HEAD; 12 of 13 T-003

  captures IN PLACE, against the explicit new-filenames instruction it
  was given. **The two prior full-scope generations (89/63 against parent
  99b8ba76, and 78/24 before it) were each likewise overwritten in place
  and are LOST — no artifact for either survives anywhere in this bundle
  or this worktree's own git history. Recorded here as a fact, not
  reconstructed.** A genuine full-scope RED was re-captured: scratch

## Confirmation-Panel Remediation (round 2, 2026-08-24)

Closes the six-verdict re-confirmation panel's remaining T-003 Majors and
two named reversals (the panel returned zero Criticals). Per-item fix,
kill naming, and counts:

- **Reversal 1 — un-forward warn diagnostics on the steps-7/8 abort
  paths** (both vendors' Major, T-003 scope). Ground-truth check:
  `requirements.md:526-528` reads verbatim "A `dsl-warn-on-matched-
  capability` id therefore never appears with **only** `severity: "warn"`
  entries and no `severity: "block"` summary entry, and no other id ever
  carries `severity: "warn"` at all (AC-056)." On the three steps-7/8
  abort paths (`registry-validation-failed`, `dependency-subprocess-
  failed`, `dependency-output-malformed`), `main()` was forwarding the
  already-collected `warn_diagnostics` into `_block(...)` even though the
  Block reached there carries a DIFFERENT id -- producing exactly the
  forbidden shape (`dsl-warn-on-matched-capability` warn entries with no
  same-id block summary, since step 9's own summary is never reached on
  an abort). The prior in-code comment defending this ("does not collide
  ... since only ONE id ever carries severity: block") answered a
  different clause of the same sentence than the one it broke. Reverted:
  the three abort `_block(...)` calls no longer pass `warn_diagnostics`
  -- collected warns are dropped, not forwarded, on a non-dsl-warn-summary
  Block. `evaluate-predicate-failure-after-warn`'s own expected shape
  updated to match (its own `capability_evaluations` half, an
  independent field REQ-004/AC-024 govern separately from `diagnostics[]`,
  is unaffected -- cap-warn-first's WARN evaluation still legitimately
  appears there). Mutation-kill (scratch clone, reverting to the
  forwarding shape -- i.e. re-passing `warn_diagnostics` into the three
  abort `_block` calls): baseline 205/0 → mutant 204/1, exactly
  `evaluate-predicate-failure-after-warn`'s own "exact Resolver Evidence"
  assertion, zero collateral -- the mutant's own actual `diagnostics[]`
  shows precisely the forbidden shape (a warn entry under one id, the
  block summary under a different id). Retained at
  `verification/qg/T-003/mutation-kill-reversal1-evaluate-predicate-
  failure-after-warn.log`.
- **Stale GREEN naming** (Anthropic Major 1): `verification/T-003/
  green-{sh,ps1}.log` re-captured in place, now genuinely byte-identical
  to `verification/qg/T-003/focused-tests-{sh,ps1}.log` (both **205
  passed, 0 failed**, sha256 `f312ed07ef31a9e01a10aba45d5aba9b4d1f0259759
  769ddc236a3e985b9b121`). The report's "Test Evidence Path" now points
  at the qg path directly (see "## Test Evidence" above) rather than
  naming a path whose own claimed equality with the qg path was false at
  review time.
- **Dependency stderr swallowed** (Anthropic Major 2). Ground-truth
  check: `requirements.md` REQ-005 (~line 591) states "a dependency
  subprocess's own stderr remains visible to a human operator on the
  terminal exactly as that subprocess itself already writes it, but
  never participates in this feature's own byte-identity comparison";
  `design.md` API/Contract Plan step 4 (~line 838) repeats this verbatim
  ("the underlying script's own stderr remains visible to a human
  operator on the terminal, exactly as resolve-component-paths itself
  already writes it; it is simply never copied into this Resolver's own
  structured `detail` field"); `security-spec.md` B5's own mitigation
  column matches. All three require terminal visibility while forbidding
  only the STRUCTURED `detail` field from quoting it verbatim -- the
  implementation was doing the latter but not the former (`stderr=
  subprocess.PIPE` captured on all four dependency calls, never
  re-emitted anywhere). Fixed: new `_reemit_dependency_stderr(result)`
  helper, writing the dependency's own raw captured bytes verbatim to
  this process's own stderr (via `sys.stderr.buffer`, never decoded/
  re-encoded), called immediately after each of the four dependency
  subprocess calls (`_run_resolve_component_paths`, `_validate_
  capability_registry`, `_generate_registry_digest_whole`,
  `_invoke_evaluate_predicate`) succeeds launching, always BEFORE the
  eventual canonical `_block`/`_block_no_write` line -- so the canonical
  line stays the LAST line on stderr, matching design.md's own ordering.
  The `run_t003_case`/`run_t004_case` fixture assertion changed from
  `stdout == "" and stderr == expected_line and "FIXTURE_INJECTED_
  FAILURE" not in stderr` (asserting the pass-through this fix now
  implements would FAIL) to `stdout == "" and stderr.endswith(expected_
  line)` (canonical line present and last; a silent dependency still
  passes trivially). Mutation-kill: dropping the `_reemit_dependency_
  stderr` call would make a stub's own known `FIXTURE_INJECTED_FAILURE`
  line vanish from stderr entirely; a NEW, targeted exact-equality
  assertion (`stderr == <known upstream line> + expected_line`) added
  for the three fixtures whose own stub dependencies inject stderr
  (`affected-component-resolution-failed`, `dependency-subprocess-
  failed`, `evaluate-predicate-failure-after-warn` in the block suite;
  `recheck-dependency-failed` in the T-004 suite, since step 13's own
  recheck reuses the identical `_run_resolve_component_paths` call site)
  -- `endswith` alone cannot catch this regression (an empty prefix still
  "ends with" the canonical line), which is why the exact-equality
  companion assertion exists specifically to kill it.
- **Minor closed — temp-file leak**: `_evaluate_predicate`'s own two
  `tempfile.mkstemp` calls previously both executed before either was in
  a `try`/`finally`; the second mkstemp's own `try`/`finally` is now
  nested inside the first's, so a failure allocating the SECOND temp
  file still cleans up the first (previously leaked for the process's
  own lifetime).
- **Minor closed — unanchored stderr-token classifications**: both
  `PREDICATE_SCHEMA_ERROR` (in `_invoke_evaluate_predicate`) and
  `canonicalizer-failed` (in `_generate_registry_digest_whole`) were
  whole-stream substring searches, so Registry/predicate content a
  dependency happens to echo back mid-line could steer the
  classification. Anchored to each dependency's own fixed line-start
  format: `line.startswith("PREDICATE_SCHEMA_ERROR")` (matches
  `evaluate-predicate.py`'s own `print(f"PREDICATE_SCHEMA_ERROR: ...")`
  calls, always line-initial) and `line.startswith("generate-registry-
  digest: canonicalizer-failed")` (matches `generate-registry-digest.py`'s
  own sole stderr `print` call's fixed prefix) via a per-line scan, never
  a whole-stream `in` check.
- **Minor closed — traceability.log stale line numbers**: the prior two
  "Superseded" paragraphs corrected fixture counts/totals but never the
  live "Implemented:" bullet's own cited line numbers (`ALL_CASE_NAMES`
  276/`run_t003_case` 437, both stale since the 2026-08-23 cycle-3 pass
  actually moved them). This round fixes the LIVE bullet directly (308/
  477, current) instead of appending a fourth Superseded paragraph that
  would repeat the same defect -- see the log's own new closing note.
- **Minors recorded, not fixed this round** (per instruction, "Leave the
  rest recorded"): the silent-`{}`-default on a missing Context
  Projection entry (`_evaluate_capabilities`); the hand-narrowed
  property-subset regression-coverage gap in two `dsl-warn-*` fixtures;

## Fourth-Round Cross-Model Panel Remediation (2026-08-25)

The third confirmation panel returned six verdicts / thirty findings
(T-003 NEEDS_WORK x2, T-004 PASS + NEEDS_WORK, T-005 NEEDS_WORK x2). This
pass began with triage against the CURRENT tree rather than with
remediation, because the panel's own recurring complaint was that it kept
reviewing evidence one round staler than the code. The triage found that
to be true again, and found a NEW instance of it that no finding could
have named because it postdates them.

### The triage result, before any remediation

The panel read its bundles at 2026-08-24 03:46-04:00. Three commits
landed after that:

* `92f21702` (06:37) -- the bookkeeping-lag pass, which closed a large
  share of the thirty findings and diagnosed the structural cause (the
  canonical `## Outputs` table was frozen at first-commit content while
  every later round appended a `### Outputs (round N)` sub-table the
  bundle assembler's parser never reads).
* `d5701194` / `b572fcd6` (07:22-07:23) -- HUMAN-APPROVED amendments to
  requirements.md: ruling A(1) added AC-056's "or jointly caused"
  exception, ruling B(1) scoped AC-016's byte-identity criterion to
  exclude `state` and the two enforcement-derived `context_binding`
  digests.
* `18d90c67` (07:30) -- the production follow-through for ruling A(1),
  restoring warn forwarding on all three steps-7/8 abort paths.

`18d90c67` changed `resolve-project-context.py`,
`resolve-project-context-block-check.py` and
`resolve-project-context-match-check.py` and regenerated NOTHING. The
measured consequences, each established by direct comparison rather than
inferred:

* `human-copy/MANIFEST.sha256` declared
  `437c861a...` for the staged resolver while the file on disk hashed
  `d3360c51...`. `apply-human-copy.sh` verifies that manifest at
  human-apply time and dies `MANIFEST_INVALID` on a mismatch, so the
  staged candidate set was **not appliable as it stood**. This was not
  one of the thirty findings; it was introduced after them, and it was
  the most serious thing the triage found.
* Five `## Outputs` rows across the three reports declared pre-`18d90c67`
  hashes.
* The `Current staged resolve-project-context.py sha256` sentence in each
  verification contract's own comment was stale -- the SAME class the
  Anthropic slot raised as a Major against that exact field, recurring on
  a later commit rather than persisting from the old one.

Everything else about the tree was sound: both suites reproduced
BYTE-IDENTICALLY from the current tree in both runtimes against their
committed GREEN logs, so the evidence content was current even though the
bookkeeping around it was not.

### Ordering discipline followed

Last code changes first, then freeze; then evidence re-captured against
the frozen tree only; then bookkeeping derived once from the result. All
GREEN and RED captures below post-date every code change in this pass.

### Findings closed by this pass (T-003)

**Anthropic Minor -- `sys.path.insert(0, script_dir)` never restored.**
Disclosed-not-fixed for four consecutive rounds on a
`Security-Sensitive: true` task. Fixed. `_discover_registry`'s body moves
to `_discover_registry_with_sibling_module()`; the caller keeps the
prepend scoped to that one call and takes it back off in a `finally`, so
every exit path restores it -- clean return, `ContractDiscoveryFailed`,
or anything unexpected. Removal is by VALUE via `list.remove`, which
drops only the FIRST occurrence, so a caller that already had
`script_dir` on `sys.path` (the common case) keeps its own entry.

Three new assertions, `run_sys_path_hygiene_check`, white-box against the
same staged file every other check runs, loaded through
`importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` -- the identical discipline
`run_draft7_validator_keyword_checks` already established for a property
with no CLI-observable trigger. The resolver exits before any later
import could be hijacked, so no black-box subprocess fixture can observe
this at all.

Mutation kill (drop the `try`/`finally`, i.e. exactly the pre-fix code),
isolated scratch copy, BOTH RUNTIMES:

    baseline  bash 219/0   pwsh 219/0
    mutant    bash 216/3   pwsh 216/3
    restored  bash 219/0   pwsh 219/0

Exactly the three new assertions flip, zero collateral. The third one
reports `occurrences=2` under the mutant -- the caller's own entry and
the function's duplicate both surviving -- which is the specific shape
assertions (1) and (2) alone could not distinguish. Full transcript:
`verification/qg/T-003/mutation-kill-sys-path-restore.log`.

A defect in this pass's OWN new test, found by the RED reconstruction and
recorded rather than quietly fixed: the first version let a non-
`ContractDiscoveryFailed` exception escape, which at the T-004 rewind
baseline (whose `_discover_registry` predates the broad `except
Exception` widening) killed the whole driver with a raw `RuntimeError`
before it could print its RESULT line. The helper now catches broadly and
reports the exception's class name as a clean FAIL -- same rule the rest
of this driver already follows.

**Anthropic Minor -- `regression.log` header stale, `red_evidence` from
an older driver, mutation-kill logs unreachable, `green-{sh,ps1}.log`
byte-identical to the sh capture:** all four were closed by `92f21702`
and re-verified here against the current tree rather than taken on
trust -- the runtime markers are present and the sh/ps1 captures now
differ; the `include_untracked=True` call site exists at
`tests/resolve-project-context-block-check.py:781`; the three
mutation-kill logs are on disk and in the canonical Outputs table.

**Anthropic Minor -- `projection_components.get(component_id, {})`
evaluates an absent Context-Projection entry against an empty properties
document instead of Blocking.** Still open, and still for the reason the
reviewer themself gave: REQ-002's diagnostic enum is closed at sixteen
values and no row covers this condition, so adding a Block would itself
be spec drift on a frozen document. Carried in Unresolved Items.

### Finding rejected, with the evidence (T-003)

**OpenAI Major -- "a capability entry is appended only after all of its
evaluations finish, so evaluations already performed for a capability
that fails midway disappear from the Resolver Evidence, contrary to
REQ-004."** Rejected.

REQ-004 binds each `capability_evaluations[]` entry to carry **exactly
one** `trigger_evaluations[]` element per affected component, and AC-018
restates that as an exact-set rule. A partially-evaluated capability
cannot satisfy it: in the `evaluate-predicate-failure-after-warn` fixture
the aborting capability `cap-fails-second` would carry ZERO trigger
evaluations against ONE affected component. On an abort the array is
necessarily incomplete either way, because the capabilities after the
failing one are never evaluated at all -- so appending the partial entry
would ADD a second, per-entry cardinality violation without curing the
first, while omitting it keeps every entry that IS present well-formed.
The fixture's exact-equality expectation encodes precisely that choice.

The other half of the same reviewer's concern was real and IS closed, by
a route outside this pass: warn diagnostics collected before an abort
were being dropped, human ruling A(1) amended AC-056 to make the lossless
shape lawful (`d5701194`), and `18d90c67` restored the forwarding in
production.

The current, authoritative capture for this task. Every earlier
`### GREEN` sub-section in this file is historical, whatever it calls
itself.

- PowerShell: exit 0, **219 passed / 0 failed**.

  (re-captured in place after this pass's own last code change, both

### Correction to this pass's own reported finding: cross-model has TWO layers (2026-08-25)

This pass reported that `Security-Sensitive` "appears nowhere in the policy
or gate scripts" and concluded it does not drive cross-model verification.
The first half is true; the conclusion is WRONG, and the over-broad claim
was relayed to other lanes before it was caught. Recorded here because the
next reader needs both layers, not the one this pass checked.

Two independent controls exist, at different layers:

1. **Contract-level** -- `risk-gate-matrix.md:42-43` and
   `cross-model-verification-policy.md` "Risk-Tier Enforcement". Activates
   ONLY when a contract declares the `cross_model` descriptor. None of
   T-003/T-004/T-005 declares it, so all three are treated as `legacy` (no
   enforcement) and `check-contract` passes all three. Verified directly;
   this half holds.

2. **Ship-driver-level** -- `plugins/sdd-ship/skills/ship/SKILL.md:295-322`,
   Step 4 "Full track", item 1. Keyed on `tasks.md`, NOT on the contract:
   a task whose tasks.md entry carries `Risk: critical` or
   `Security-Sensitive: true` MUST have cross-model verification run in the
   same ship invocation before it reaches the quality gate, "regardless of
   whether `--verify` was passed", with an explicit FAIL-CLOSED STOP when
   there is neither a completed run in that invocation nor a valid
   `Cross-Model-Waiver:`. A waiver is valid only alongside a co-located
   human approval mark naming a second distinct approver; no task in this
   feature records any `Cross-Model-Waiver:` at all.

The lesson worth keeping: "not implemented in a shell script" is not the
same as "not enforced". The ship skill IS the gate driver, and a control
written into the driver's own procedure binds exactly as much as one
written into `check-contract`. Layer 1 was checked, layer 2 was not, and
the resulting claim travelled further than the evidence behind it.

Consequence for this feature, from both layers together:

* T-003 and T-004 carry `Security-Sensitive: true`. Layer 2's fail-closed
  stop applies to both: neither can reach the quality gate until
  cross-model verification runs in the same ship invocation. Both are also
  INELIGIBLE for the lite track (`ship/SKILL.md:373-381` -- "the lite track
  has no cross-model step").
* T-005 carries `Security-Sensitive: false` and `Risk: medium`. Neither
  layer requires cross-model for it, so its stale NEEDS_WORK panel verdicts
  are advisory rather than gating for that task.

Independently of both layers, `ship` and `quality-gate` each carry
`disable-model-invocation: true` (and `quality-gate` additionally
`user-invocable: false`, "invoked by sdd-ship. Do not invoke directly"), so
the gate is human-invoked for every task regardless of tier.

### v0.3.0 からの移行

| v0.3.0 | v0.4.0 |
|---|---|
| Claude Code のみ対応 | **Copilot CLI対応**: SKILL.md スキル、`*.agent.md` エージェント、`hooks/copilot-hooks.json` (preToolUse、既知の不具合: サブエージェント内) |
| Codex hookなし / エージェントなし | **Codex hooks/agents対応**: `command_windows` フィールド追加、`apply_patch` ペイロード処理、`.codex/agents/` TOML エージェント、インストーラーが `~/.codex/agents/` へコピー |
| 個別の kill-switch / guard スクリプト | **統一ガード `sdd-hook-guard`**: 3ランタイム共通、kill-switch + タスク承認チェックを統合 |
| check-contract / check-task-state 基本版 | **強化版スクリプト**: `waiver_reason` 必須化、証拠パストラバーサル防止、重複タスクID検出、実装レポート必須 (`Implementation Complete`)、`Blocked`/`Done` 検証強化、ベースライン必須セット保護 |
| CIテンプレートがコマンドなしで通過 | **フェイルクローズ化**: `TODO_REPLACE_WITH_PROJECT_COMMANDS` マーカーで未設定のまま通過しない |

## v0.3.0

### 新機能 (参考)

**調査フェーズ (investigate-codebase)**: `sdd-investigator` エージェントが読み取り専用でコードを解析し、`INV-xxx` 知見と `BL-xxx` 基線動作を生成する。

**refactorモード**: `sdd-bootstrap-interviewer` に追加。`baseline-behavior.md` が必須前提となり、受入条件を BL 同値として表現する。

**決定論的検証ゲート**: Default-FAIL 契約 (`verification-contract.json`)、`check-contract` / `check-placeholders` / `check-task-state` スクリプト (.sh / .ps1) により、エージェント自己申告に依存しない機械検証を実施する。

**独立 Evaluator**: `sdd-evaluator` サブエージェント (Claude Code) または新規セッション (Codex) が実装文脈を持たない状態で批判レビューを行う。

**Claude Code フック強制層**: `PreToolUse` フックがキルスイッチ (AGENT_STOP) と自己承認の強制ブロックを行う (現在は `hooks/claude-hooks.json` + `kill-switch.js` / `sdd-hook-guard.js` の Node.js exec form に移行済み)。

**ワークフローレトロスペクティブ**: `workflow-retrospective` スキルがリワーク指標を計測し、WFI (Workflow Improvement) 提案を人間承認ループで適用する。

### v0.2.0 からの移行

| v0.2.0 | v0.3.0 |
|---|---|
| bootstrap は3モード (project/feature/bugfix) | 4モード (+ `refactor`) |
| 調査フェーズなし | `investigate-codebase` (Stage 0) が追加 |
| `quality-gate` はエージェント自己申告を許容 | Default-FAIL 検証契約 + 決定論的スクリプトゲート |
| 独立レビューは任意 | `sdd-evaluator` サブエージェント (または新規セッション) が必須 |
| フックなし | `hooks/hooks.json` が承認ガード / AGENT_STOP を強制 |
| レトロスペクティブなし | `workflow-retrospective` + WFI ループが追加 |

#![PATH_REDACTED] python3
"""Capability Resolver, implementation stages T-002+T-003+T-004 (steps 0-13).

T-002 validates CLI input, derives Resolver state, obtains a single canonical
Project Context snapshot, and assembles/canonicalizes the Context Projection
in memory (steps 0-3). T-003 extends the same Python master with
affected-component resolution (`resolve-component-paths`), Registry
discovery + validation (ADR-0025 discovery + `validate-capability-registry`),
`registry_digest` (`generate-registry-digest --whole`), per-Capability/
per-affected-component trigger evaluation, matched-Capability
conditional-facet evaluation (both via `evaluate-predicate`), and the
any-branch WARN check (steps 4-9). T-004 completes the staged-only pipeline:
the track branch (Facet Manifest on `full`, Capability Summary on `lite`,
never both -- B4), Resolver Evidence assembly (context_binding/resolver
provenance canonicalization, B9), output schema self-validation against
every staged artifact's own governing schema (B3), and the pre-publication
snapshot recheck (B8 TOCTOU) (steps 10-13). Every step 0-13 result is staged
in memory only -- a clean resolve (exit 0) writes nothing to any live path;
T-007 layers the crash-recovery scan and the journaled publication
transaction (step 14) onto this same script, the sole component with any
live-filesystem write of its own.
"""

import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile


EXIT_BLOCK = 1
EXIT_USAGE = 2
FEATURE_RE = re.compile(r"^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$")
EVIDENCE_SCHEMA = "sdd-resolver-evidence/v1"

# T-004 (steps 10-13): resolver.version/resolver.rule_set_revision
[... 83965 bytes elided from the middle of specs/epic-193-a5-capability-resolver/human-copy/plugins/sdd-quality-loop/scripts/resolve-project-context.py (original size 87449 bytes; showing first/last 40 lines) ...]
            context_binding=context_binding, resolver_block=resolver_block,
        )
    except DependencySubprocessFailed:
        detail = (
            "a dependency subprocess failed while re-deriving affected components or registry_digest "
            "during the pre-publication recheck"
        )
        return _block(
            repo_root, args.feature, "dependency-subprocess-failed", detail, state, capability_evaluations,
            context_binding=context_binding, resolver_block=resolver_block,
        )
    except DependencyOutputMalformed:
        detail = (
            "a dependency subprocess returned malformed output while re-deriving affected components or "
            "registry_digest during the pre-publication recheck"
        )
        return _block(
            repo_root, args.feature, "dependency-output-malformed", detail, state, capability_evaluations,
            context_binding=context_binding, resolver_block=resolver_block,
        )
    except SnapshotGenerationMismatch:
        detail = (
            "a pre-publication recheck of the Project Context, Registry, or "
            "ownership-source snapshot detected drift since this invocation's own snapshot"
        )
        return _block(
            repo_root, args.feature, "snapshot-generation-mismatch", detail, state, capability_evaluations,
            context_binding=context_binding, resolver_block=resolver_block,
        )

    # T-004 owns staging only through step 13 -- step 14's own journaled
    # publication transaction (T-007's own scope) is the sole component
    # with any live-filesystem write of its own. A clean resolve here
    # writes nothing to any live path.
    del track_artifact, evidence
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

# ---- specs/epic-193-a5-capability-resolver/verification/qg/T-003/mutation-kill-sys-path-restore.log (declared output) ----
Mutation kill: `_discover_registry`'s own `sys.path` try/finally restore
(T-003 scope, 2026-08-25 fourth-round cross-model panel remediation).

FINDING CLOSED. Anthropic slot, raised against BOTH T-003 and T-004 and
re-raised unclosed in every round since: "sys.path.insert(0,
str(script_dir)) is never restored (no try/finally, no importlib.util.
spec_from_file_location), so any later stdlib-shadowing module in the
deployed scripts directory would win import resolution for the rest of
the process. Disclosed, unfixed across three rounds on a
Security-Sensitive task."

FIX. `_discover_registry`'s body is split into
`_discover_registry_with_sibling_module()`; the caller keeps the
`sys.path` prepend scoped to exactly that call and takes it back off in a
`finally`, so every exit path -- clean return, ContractDiscoveryFailed,
or any unexpected exception -- restores it. Removal is by VALUE via
`list.remove`, which drops only the FIRST occurrence, so a caller that
already had `script_dir` on `sys.path` keeps its own entry.

NEW ASSERTIONS (three, tests/resolve-project-context-block-check.py,
`run_sys_path_hygiene_check`). White-box against the SAME staged file
every other check runs, loaded via `importlib.util.spec_from_file_
location` -- the identical discipline `run_draft7_validator_keyword_
checks` already established for a property with no CLI-observable
trigger. The resolver exits before any later import could be hijacked, so
no black-box subprocess fixture can observe this property.
  (1) clean return restores sys.path exactly;
  (2) the ContractDiscoveryFailed path restores it too (proving the
      `finally`, not a lucky fall-through on the success path);
  (3) a caller's pre-existing identical sys.path entry SURVIVES the call
      (the first-occurrence-only removal discipline).

MUTANT. Drop the `try`/`finally` entirely -- `return
_discover_registry_with_sibling_module()` with the prepend left standing,
i.e. exactly the pre-fix code the finding describes.

KILL, isolated scratch copy, collateral-free, BOTH RUNTIMES:

"""T-003 fixture stub: deterministically returns `affected_components` in a

never assumed from upstream.

by T-005's `resolve-project-context-match-check.py`, outside this bundle.
This fixture's own return value is unaffected by `--include-untracked`

wiring `run_t003_case` to call it with `include_untracked=True` costs

from pathlib import Path

#![PATH_REDACTED] python3
"""Projection-pass capture stub for T-002's step 3 assertions.

The resolver's step 2 must behave exactly as in production, so every
YAML-mode invocation is delegated verbatim to the real canonicalizer, which
the driver copies beside this stub as `canonicalize-sdd-yaml-real.py`.

The step 3 (JSON-mode) invocation is the one under observation. Its input
file is the resolver's own in-memory Context Projection, serialized to a
temporary path -- the only point at which that structure is observable from
outside the process, since T-002 stages it and never writes a live artifact
(design.md step 3, B1/B4). The stub copies those bytes byte-for-byte to
`<fixture-repo>/projection-capture.json` before doing anything else, then
behaves per SDD_T002_PROJECTION_MODE:

  passthrough   delegate to the real canonicalizer (success path)
  json-fail     exit non-zero  -> canonicalizer-invocation-failed
  json-garbage  exit 0, unparseable stdout -> dependency-output-malformed

The two failure modes emit an `UPSTREAM_SECRET` marker so the caller can
assert M8 (upstream stderr is never copied into the Resolver's own
diagnostic). There is no default mode: an unset variable fails closed.

T-003 note: once the resolver's own step 6 (`registry_digest`) lands, a
SECOND, later JSON-mode canonicalizer invocation reaches this same stub (via
`generate-registry-digest --whole`'s own identical canonicalizer call) after
step 3's own capture has already happened. This stub captures only the
FIRST JSON-mode call it sees per process tree, so step 3's own capture is
never clobbered by that later, unrelated call -- it still delegates every
JSON-mode call (including that later one) to the real canonicalizer either
way.
"""
import os
from pathlib import Path
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
REAL = HERE / "canonicalize-sdd-yaml-real.py"
# HERE is <fixture-repo>/plugins/sdd-quality-loop/scripts.
CAPTURE = HERE.parents[2] / "projection-capture.json"


def delegate(argv):
    result = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, str(REAL), *argv],
        stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, check=False,
    )
    sys.stdout.buffer.write(result.stdout)
    sys.stdout.buffer.flush()
    sys.stderr.buffer.write(result.stderr)
    sys.stderr.buffer.flush()
    return result.returncode


def main():
    argv = sys.argv[1:]
    json_mode = (
        "--input-format" in argv
        and argv.index("--input-format") + 1 < len(argv)
        and argv[argv.index("--input-format") + 1] == "json"
    )
    if not json_mode:
        return delegate(argv)

    if not CAPTURE.exists():
        shutil.copyfile(argv[0], str(CAPTURE))
    mode = os.environ.get("SDD_T002_PROJECTION_MODE", "")
    if mode == "passthrough":
        return delegate(argv)
    if mode == "json-fail":
        sys.stderr.write("UPSTREAM_SECRET: platform-specific canonicalizer failure\n")
        return 27
    if mode == "json-garbage":
        sys.stdout.write("UPSTREAM_SECRET:not-json")
        return 0
    sys.stderr.write("stub: SDD_T002_PROJECTION_MODE unset or unknown\n")
    return 90


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

    Pop-Location

    foreach ($failedTest in $failed) {
        Write-Host "  $failedTest"
    }
    exit 1
}
